```json
    "content": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Generate Figure 2: EC-HTE method overview (concept diagram).\nDesigned for EMNLP figure* (full textwidth ≈ 6.3in). figsize=10in → ~63% scale.\nAll font sizes chosen so printed text ≥ 7pt.\n\"\"\"\nimport matplotlib\nmatplotlib.use('Agg')\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\nfrom matplotlib.patches import FancyBboxPatch, FancyArrowPatch, Circle\nimport matplotlib.patheffects as pe\n\n# ─── Figure ───\nFW, FH = 10, 3.9\nfig = plt.figure(figsize=(FW, FH), dpi=300, facecolor='white')\nax = fig.add_axes([0, 0, 1, 1])\nax.set_xlim(0, FW)\nax.set_ylim(0, FH)\nax.axis('off')\nax.set_facecolor('white')\n\nplt.rcParams.update({'mathtext.fontset': 'cm', 'font.family': 'DejaVu Serif'})\n\n# ─── Colors (muted academic) ───\nC = dict(\n    std_band='#FBE9E7',  std_fill='#FFCCBC',  std_border='#BF360C',\n    std_text='#BF360C',  std_accent='#D84315',\n    ours_band='#E0F2F1', ours_fill='#B2DFDB', ours_border='#00695C',\n    ours_text='#00695C',  ours_accent='#00897B',\n    input_fill='#E3F2FD', input_border='#1565C0', input_text='#0D47A1',\n    header='#263238', label='#546E7A', anno='#78909C',\n    result_good='#00897B', diag_fill='#FFF8E1', diag_border='#F9A825',\n    diag_text='#E65100',\n)\n\n# ─── Layout ───\ncol = dict(input=1.3, est=3.7, corr=6.3, inf=8.9)\nheader_y = 3.65\nstd_y = 2.90\nvs_y = 2.22\nours_y = 1.28\nresult_y = 0.22\n\nBW, BH = 1.95, 0.58\nIW, IH = 1.55, 0.38\n\n# ─── Helpers ───\ndef rbox(x, y, w, h, fill, border, lw=1.2):\n    shadow = FancyBboxPatch((x - w/2 + 0.02, y - h/2 - 0.018), w, h,\n        boxstyle=\"round,pad=0.08\", facecolor='#00000008', edgecolor='none', zorder=1)\n    ax.add_patch(shadow)\n    box = FancyBboxPatch((x - w/2, y - h/2), w, h, boxstyle=\"round,pad=0.08\",\n        facecolor=fill, edgecolor=border, linewidth=lw, zorder=2)\n    ax.add_patch(box)\n\ndef btext(x, y, main, sub=None, color='black', ms=14, ss=10.5):\n    if sub:\n        ax.text(x, y + 0.09, main, ha='center', va='center', fontsize=ms,\n                fontweight='bold', color=color, zorder=5)\n        ax.text(x, y - 0.11, sub, ha='center', va='center', fontsize=ss,\n                color=color, alpha=0.6, fontstyle='italic', zorder=5)\n    else:\n        ax.text(x, y, main, ha='center', va='center', fontsize=ms,\n                fontweight='bold', color=color, zorder=5)\n\ndef arr(x1, y1, x2, y2, color='#90A4AE', lw=1.0, ls='-'):\n    a = FancyArrowPatch((x1, y1), (x2, y2),\n        arrowstyle='->,head_width=2.5,head_length=2.5',\n        color=color, linewidth=lw, linestyle=ls, zorder=3, mutation_scale=10)\n    ax.add_patch(a)\n\ndef icon_cross(x, y, bg):\n    ax.add_patch(Circle((x, y), 0.12, facecolor=bg, edgecolor='white', lw=1.3, zorder=6))\n    d = 0.05\n    ax.plot([x-d, x+d], [y-d, y+d], color='white', lw=1.8, solid_capstyle='round', zorder=7)\n    ax.plot([x-d, x+d], [y+d, y-d], color='white', lw=1.8, solid_capstyle='round', zorder=7)\n\ndef icon_check(x, y, bg):\n    ax.add_patch(Circle((x, y), 0.12, facecolor=bg, edgecolor='white', lw=1.3, zorder=6))\n    ax.plot([x-0.045, x-0.005, x+0.06], [y-0.005, y-0.05, y+0.05],\n            color='white', lw=1.8, solid_capstyle='round', solid_joinstyle='round', zorder=7)\n\n# ─── Background Bands ───\nax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((0.35, 2.55), FW - 0.7, 0.76,\n    boxstyle=\"round,pad=0.1\", facecolor=C['std_band'], edgecolor='none', zorder=0, alpha=0.55))\nax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((0.35, 0.6), FW - 0.7, 1.55,\n    boxstyle=\"round,pad=0.1\", facecolor=C['ours_band'], edgecolor='none', zorder=0, alpha=0.45))\n\n# ─── Column Headers ───\nfor label, x in [('Input', col['input']), ('Estimation', col['est']),\n                  ('Correction', col['corr']), ('Inference', col['inf'])]:\n    ax.text(x, header_y, label, ha='center', va='center', fontsize=16,\n            fontweight='bold', color=C['header'], zorder=5)\nax.plot([0.5, FW - 0.5], [header_y - 0.15, header_y - 0.15],\n        color=C['header'], lw=0.4, alpha=0.2, zorder=1)\n\n# ─── Row Labels ───\nax.text(0.55, std_y, 'Standard', ha='center', va='center', fontsize=12,\n        fontweight='bold', color=C['std_accent'], rotation=90, zorder=5)\nax.text(0.42, ours_y, 'EC-HTE\\n(Ours)', ha='center', va='center', fontsize=11,\n        fontweight='bold', color=C['ours_accent'], rotation=90, zorder=5,\n        linespacing=0.85)\n\n# ─── \"vs.\" ───\nax.text(FW / 2, vs_y, 'vs.', ha='center', va='center', fontsize=16,\n        fontweight='bold', color=C['label'], zorder=5)\n\n# ═══════ STANDARD PATH ═══════\nrbox(col['est'], std_y, BW, BH, C['std_fill'], C['std_border'])\nbtext(col['est'], std_y, r'Pooled CM $\\hat{C}$', 'One matrix for all', C['std_text'])\n\nrbox(col['corr'], std_y, BW, BH, C['std_fill'], C['std_border'])\nbtext(col['corr'], std_y, 'Global Correction', r'$\\hat{C}^{-1}$ applied uniformly', C['std_text'])\n\nrbox(col['inf'], std_y, BW, BH, '#FFCDD2', C['std_accent'], lw=1.6)\nbtext(col['inf'], std_y, 'Sign Reversal', '14.9% of configs', C['std_accent'])\nicon_cross(col['inf'] + BW/2 - 0.03, std_y + BH/2 - 0.03, C['std_accent'])\n\narr(col['est'] + BW/2 + 0.06, std_y, col['corr'] - BW/2 - 0.1, std_y,\n    color=C['std_accent'], lw=1.0)\narr(col['corr'] + BW/2 + 0.06, std_y, col['inf'] - BW/2 - 0.1, std_y,\n    color=C['std_accent'], lw=1.0)\n\n# ═══════ EC-HTE PATH ═══════\niy_top = ours_y + 0.27\niy_bot = ours_y - 0.27\n\nrbox(col['input'], iy_top, IW, IH, C['input_fill'], C['input_border'], lw=0.9)\nax.text(col['input'], iy_top + 0.04, 'LLM Annotations', ha='center', va='center',\n        fontsize=12, fontweight='bold', color=C['input_text'], zorder=5)\nax.text(col['input'], iy_top - 0.1, r'$N$ noisy labels $\\hat{Z}$', ha='center',\n        va='center', fontsize=9, color=C['input_text'], alpha=0.55,\n        fontstyle='italic', zorder=5)\n\nrbox(col['input'], iy_bot, IW, IH, C['input_fill'], C['input_border'], lw=0.9)\nax.text(col['input'], iy_bot + 0.04, 'Expert Labels', ha='center', va='center',\n        fontsize=12, fontweight='bold', color=C['input_text'], zorder=5)\nax.text(col['input'], iy_bot - 0.1, r'$n_{\\mathrm{expert}}$ pairs $(Z,\\hat{Z})$',\n        ha='center', va='center', fontsize=9, color=C['input_text'], alpha=0.55,\n        fontstyle='italic', zorder=5)\n\nrbox(col['est'], ours_y, BW, BH, C['ours_fill'], C['ours_border'])\nbtext(col['est'], ours_y, 'Hierarchical Bayes', r'Per-subgroup CMs $\\{\\hat{C}_s\\}$',\n      C['ours_text'])\n\nrbox(col['corr'], ours_y, BW, BH, C['ours_fill'], C['ours_border'])\nbtext(col['corr'], ours_y, 'Per-subgroup Corr.', r'$\\tilde{\\mu}_s = \\hat{C}_s^{-1} \\cdot \\hat{\\mu}_s$',\n      C['ours_text'])\n\nrbox(col['inf'], ours_y, BW, BH, '#C8E6C9', C['ours_accent'], lw=1.6)\nbtext(col['inf'], ours_y, 'Corrected CATE', r'$\\hat{\\tau}(x) \\pm \\mathrm{SE}_{\\mathrm{corr}}$',\n      C['ours_accent'])\nicon_check(col['inf'] + BW/2 - 0.03, ours_y + BH/2 - 0.03, C['ours_accent'])\n\narr(col['input'] + IW/2 + 0.06, iy_top, col['est'] - BW/2 - 0.1, ours_y + 0.06,\n    color=C['ours_accent'], lw=0.9)\narr(col['input'] + IW/2 + 0.06, iy_bot, col['est'] - BW/2 - 0.1, ours_y - 0.06,\n    color=C['ours_accent'], lw=0.9)\narr(col['est'] + BW/2 + 0.06, ours_y, col['corr'] - BW/2 - 0.1, ours_y,\n    color=C['ours_accent'], lw=1.0)\narr(col['corr'] + BW/2 + 0.06, ours_y, col['inf'] - BW/2 - 0.1, ours_y,\n    color=C['ours_accent'], lw=1.0)\n\n# ─── \"prior\" dashed arrow ───\narr(col['est'], std_y - BH/2 - 0.04, col['est'], ours_y + BH/2 + 0.08,\n    color=C['label'], lw=0.8, ls='--')\nt = ax.text(col['est'] + 0.15, vs_y, 'prior', ha='left', va='center',\n            fontsize=11, color=C['anno'], fontstyle='italic', zorder=5)\nt.set_path_effects([pe.withStroke(linewidth=2.5, foreground='white')])\n\n# ─── Annotations below EC-HTE boxes ───\nay = ours_y - BH/2 - 0.12\nax.text(col['est'], ay, r'Partial pooling · $\\alpha_0$ via Emp. Bayes',\n        ha='center', va='top', fontsize=9.5, color=C['anno'], zorder=5)\nax.text(col['corr'], ay, r'$\\kappa$-guard: $\\kappa > 100 \\rightarrow$ fallback',\n        ha='center', va='top', fontsize=9.5, color=C['anno'], zorder=5)\nax.text(col['inf'], ay, 'via CausalForestDML', ha='center', va='top',\n        fontsize=9.5, color=C['anno'], zorder=5)\n\n# ─── Bottom results ───\nax.text(FW / 2 - 1.3, result_y, r'$\\mathbf{0\\%}$ sign reversal  ·  |bias| $\\div$ 7.4',\n        ha='center', va='center', fontsize=13, fontweight='bold',\n        color=C['result_good'], zorder=5)\n\ndw, dh = 3.8, 0.28\ndx = FW / 2 + 1.9\nax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((dx - dw/2, result_y - dh/2), dw, dh,\n    boxstyle=\"round,pad=0.05\", facecolor=C['diag_fill'],\n    edgecolor=C['diag_border'], linewidth=0.9, zorder=2))\nax.text(dx, result_y,\n        r'Diagnostic (Prop. 2): $|\\delta_s|/((1{-}2\\bar{\\pi})\\cdot\\pi_s) > 1$',\n        ha='center', va='center', fontsize=10.5, color=C['diag_text'], zorder=5)\n\n# ─── Save ───\nout = '/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520/projects/ec_hte_llm_annotation/docs/paper/figures'\nfig.savefig(f'{out}/fig_method_overview.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight',\n            pad_inches=0.06, facecolor='white')\nfig.savefig(f'{out}/fig_method_overview.pdf', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight',\n            pad_inches=0.06, facecolor='white')\nplt.close()\nprint('Done: fig_method_overview.png + .pdf')\n",

    "originalFile": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Generate Figure 2: EC-HTE method overview (concept diagram).\"\"\"\nimport matplotlib\nmatplotlib.use('Agg')\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\nfrom matplotlib.patches import FancyBboxPatch, FancyArrowPatch, Circle\nimport matplotlib.patheffects as pe\n\n# ─── Figure Setup ───\nfig_w, fig_h = 14, 5.2\nfig = plt.figure(figsize=(fig_w, fig_h), dpi=300, facecolor='white')\nax = fig.add_axes([0, 0, 1, 1])\nax.set_xlim(0, fig_w)\nax.set_ylim(0, fig_h)\nax.axis('off')\nax.set_facecolor('white')\n\nplt.rcParams.update({\n    'mathtext.fontset': 'cm',\n    'font.family': 'DejaVu Serif',\n})\n\n# ─── Color Palette (muted academic) ───\nC = dict(\n    std_band='#FBE9E7',  std_fill='#FFCCBC',  std_border='#BF360C',\n    std_text='#BF360C',  std_accent='#D84315',\n    ours_band='#E0F2F1', ours_fill='#B2DFDB', ours_border='#00695C',\n    ours_text='#00695C',  ours_accent='#00897B',\n    input_fill='#E3F2FD', input_border='#1565C0', input_text='#0D47A1',\n    header='#263238', label='#546E7A', anno='#78909C',\n    result_good='#00897B', diag_fill='#FFF8E1', diag_border='#F9A825',\n    diag_text='#F57F17',\n)\n\n# ─── Layout ───\ncol = dict(input=2.0, est=5.5, corr=9.0, inf=12.5)\nheader_y = 4.85\nstd_y = 3.85\nvs_y = 2.95\nours_y = 1.65\nresult_y = 0.35\n\nBW, BH = 2.55, 0.78\nIW, IH = 2.15, 0.52\n\n# ─── Helpers ───\ndef rbox(x, y, w, h, fill, border, lw=1.3):\n    shadow = FancyBboxPatch((x - w/2 + 0.03, y - h/2 - 0.025), w, h,\n                            boxstyle=\"round,pad=0.1\", facecolor='#0000000A',\n                            edgecolor='none', zorder=1)\n    ax.add_patch(shadow)\n    box = FancyBboxPatch((x - w/2, y - h/2), w, h, boxstyle=\"round,pad=0.1\",\n                         facecolor=fill, edgecolor=border, linewidth=lw, zorder=2)\n    ax.add_patch(box)\n\ndef btext(x, y, main, sub=None, color='black', ms=11, ss=8.5):\n    if sub:\n        ax.text(x, y + 0.12, main, ha='center', va='center', fontsize=ms,\n                fontweight='bold', color=color, zorder=5)\n        ax.text(x, y - 0.14, sub, ha='center', va='center', fontsize=ss,\n                color=color, alpha=0.6, fontstyle='italic', zorder=5)\n    else:\n        ax.text(x, y, main, ha='center', va='center', fontsize=ms,\n                fontweight='bold', color=color, zorder=5)\n\ndef arr(x1, y1, x2, y2, color='#90A4AE', lw=1.2, ls='-'):\n    a = FancyArrowPatch((x1, y1), (x2, y2), arrowstyle='->,head_width=3,head_length=3',\n                        color=color, linewidth=lw, linestyle=ls, zorder=3,\n                        mutation_scale=10)\n    ax.add_patch(a)\n\ndef icon_cross(x, y, bg_color):\n    r = 0.16\n    circ = Circle((x, y), r, facecolor=bg_color, edgecolor='white',\n                  linewidth=1.5, zorder=6)\n    ax.add_patch(circ)\n    d = 0.07\n    ax.plot([x-d, x+d], [y-d, y+d], color='white', lw=2, solid_capstyle='round', zorder=7)\n    ax.plot([x-d, x+d], [y+d, y-d], color='white', lw=2, solid_capstyle='round', zorder=7)\n\ndef icon_check(x, y, bg_color):\n    r = 0.16\n    circ = Circle((x, y), r, facecolor=bg_color, edgecolor='white',\n                  linewidth=1.5, zorder=6)\n    ax.add_patch(circ)\n    ax.plot([x-0.06, x-0.01, x+0.08], [y-0.01, y-0.07, y+0.07],\n            color='white', lw=2, solid_capstyle='round', solid_joinstyle='round', zorder=7)\n\n# ─── Background Bands ───\nax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((0.45, 3.35), fig_w - 0.9, 1.05,\n             boxstyle=\"round,pad=0.12\", facecolor=C['std_band'],\n             edgecolor='none', zorder=0, alpha=0.6))\nax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((0.45, 0.75), fig_w - 0.9, 1.95,\n             boxstyle=\"round,pad=0.12\", facecolor=C['ours_band'],\n             edgecolor='none', zorder=0, alpha=0.5))\n\n# ─── Column Headers ───\nfor label, x in [('Input', col['input']), ('Estimation', col['est']),\n                  ('Correction', col['corr']), ('Inference', col['inf'])]:\n    ax.text(x, header_y, label, ha='center', va='center', fontsize=13,\n            fontweight='bold', color=C['header'], zorder=5)\nax.plot([0.8, fig_w - 0.8], [header_y - 0.2, header_y - 0.2],\n        color=C['header'], lw=0.4, alpha=0.25, zorder=1)\n\n# ─── Row Labels ───\nax.text(0.78, std_y, 'Standard', ha='center', va='center', fontsize=10,\n        fontweight='bold', color=C['std_accent'], rotation=90, zorder=5)\nax.text(0.65, ours_y, 'EC-HTE (Ours)', ha='center', va='center', fontsize=10,\n        fontweight='bold', color=C['ours_accent'], rotation=90, zorder=5)\n\n# ─── \"vs.\" ───\nax.text(fig_w / 2, vs_y, 'vs.', ha='center', va='center', fontsize=13,\n        fontweight='bold', color=C['label'], zorder=5)\n\n# ═══════ STANDARD PATH ═══════\nrbox(col['est'], std_y, BW, BH, C['std_fill'], C['std_border'])\nbtext(col['est'], std_y, r'Pooled CM $\\hat{C}$', 'One matrix for all', C['std_text'])\n\nrbox(col['corr'], std_y, BW, BH, C['std_fill'], C['std_border'])\nbtext(col['corr'], std_y, 'Global Correction', r'$\\hat{C}^{-1}$ applied uniformly', C['std_text'])\n\nrbox(col['inf'], std_y, BW, BH, '#FFCDD2', C['std_accent'], lw=1.8)\nbtext(col['inf'], std_y, 'Sign Reversal', '14.9% of configs', C['std_accent'])\nicon_cross(col['inf'] + BW/2 - 0.05, std_y + BH/2 - 0.05, C['std_accent'])\n\narr(col['est'] + BW/2 + 0.08, std_y, col['corr'] - BW/2 - 0.15, std_y,\n    color=C['std_accent'], lw=1.2)\narr(col['corr'] + BW/2 + 0.08, std_y, col['inf'] - BW/2 - 0.15, std_y,\n    color=C['std_accent'], lw=1.2)\n\n# ═══════ EC-HTE PATH ═══════\niy_top = ours_y + 0.35\niy_bot = ours_y - 0.35\n\nrbox(col['input'], iy_top, IW, IH, C['input_fill'], C['input_border'], lw=1.0)\nax.text(col['input'], iy_top + 0.06, 'LLM Annotations', ha='center', va='center',\n        fontsize=9.5, fontweight='bold', color=C['input_text'], zorder=5)\nax.text(col['input'], iy_top - 0.13, r'$N$ noisy labels $\\hat{Z}$', ha='center',\n        va='center', fontsize=7.5, color=C['input_text'], alpha=0.55,\n        fontstyle='italic', zorder=5)\n\nrbox(col['input'], iy_bot, IW, IH, C['input_fill'], C['input_border'], lw=1.0)\nax.text(col['input'], iy_bot + 0.06, 'Expert Labels', ha='center', va='center',\n        fontsize=9.5, fontweight='bold', color=C['input_text'], zorder=5)\nax.text(col['input'], iy_bot - 0.13, r'$n_{\\mathrm{expert}}$ pairs $(Z,\\hat{Z})$',\n        ha='center', va='center', fontsize=7.5, color=C['input_text'], alpha=0.55,\n        fontstyle='italic', zorder=5)\n\nrbox(col['est'], ours_y, BW, BH, C['ours_fill'], C['ours_border'])\nbtext(col['est'], ours_y, 'Hierarchical Bayes', r'Per-subgroup CMs $\\{\\hat{C}_s\\}$',\n      C['ours_text'])\n\nrbox(col['corr'], ours_y, BW, BH, C['ours_fill'], C['ours_border'])\nbtext(col['corr'], ours_y, 'Per-subgroup Corr.', r'$\\tilde{\\mu}_s = \\hat{C}_s^{-1} \\cdot \\hat{\\mu}_s$',\n      C['ours_text'])\n\nrbox(col['inf'], ours_y, BW, BH, '#C8E6C9', C['ours_accent'], lw=1.8)\nbtext(col['inf'], ours_y, 'Corrected CATE', r'$\\hat{\\tau}(x) \\pm \\mathrm{SE}_{\\mathrm{corr}}$',\n      C['ours_accent'])\nicon_check(col['inf'] + BW/2 - 0.05, ours_y + BH/2 - 0.05, C['ours_accent'])\n\n# EC-HTE arrows\narr(col['input'] + IW/2 + 0.08, iy_top, col['est'] - BW/2 - 0.15, ours_y + 0.08,\n    color=C['ours_accent'], lw=1.1)\narr(col['input'] + IW/2 + 0.08, iy_bot, col['est'] - BW/2 - 0.15, ours_y - 0.08,\n    color=C['ours_accent'], lw=1.1)\narr(col['est'] + BW/2 + 0.08, ours_y, col['corr'] - BW/2 - 0.15, ours_y,\n    color=C['ours_accent'], lw=1.2)\narr(col['corr'] + BW/2 + 0.08, ours_y, col['inf'] - BW/2 - 0.15, ours_y,\n    color=C['ours_accent'], lw=1.2)\n\n# ─── \"prior\" Dashed Arrow (Pooled CM → HB) ───\narr(col['est'], std_y - BH/2 - 0.05, col['est'], ours_y + BH/2 + 0.12,\n    color=C['label'], lw=0.9, ls='--')\nt = ax.text(col['est'] + 0.22, vs_y, 'prior', ha='left', va='center',\n            fontsize=9, color=C['anno'], fontstyle='italic', zorder=5)\nt.set_path_effects([pe.withStroke(linewidth=2.5, foreground='white')])\n\n# ─── Annotations Below EC-HTE Boxes ───\nanno_y = ours_y - BH/2 - 0.17\nax.text(col['est'], anno_y, r'Partial pooling · $\\alpha_0$ via Emp. Bayes',\n        ha='center', va='top', fontsize=7.5, color=C['anno'], zorder=5)\nax.text(col['corr'], anno_y, r'$\\kappa$-guard: $\\kappa > 100 \\rightarrow$ fallback',\n        ha='center', va='top', fontsize=7.5, color=C['anno'], zorder=5)\nax.text(col['inf'], anno_y, 'via CausalForestDML', ha='center', va='top',\n        fontsize=7.5, color=C['anno'], zorder=5)\n\n# ─── Bottom Results ───\nax.text(fig_w / 2 - 1.8, result_y, '0% sign reversal  ·  |bias| ÷ 7.4',\n        ha='center', va='center', fontsize=10, fontweight='bold',\n        color=C['result_good'], zorder=5)\n\ndw, dh = 5.0, 0.38\ndx = fig_w / 2 + 2.2\nax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((dx - dw/2, result_y - dh/2), dw, dh,\n             boxstyle=\"round,pad=0.06\", facecolor=C['diag_fill'],\n             edgecolor=C['diag_border'], linewidth=1.0, zorder=2))\nax.text(dx, result_y,\n        r'Diagnostic (Prop. 2):  $|\\delta_s| / ((1-2\\bar{\\pi}) \\cdot \\pi_s) > 1$',\n        ha='center', va='center', fontsize=8.5, color=C['diag_text'], zorder=5)\n\n# ─── Save ───\nout = '/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0520/projects/ec_hte_llm_annotation/docs/paper/figures'\nfig.savefig(f'{out}/fig_method_overview.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight',\n            pad_inches=0.08, facecolor='white')\nfig.savefig(f'{out}/fig_method_overview.pdf', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight',\n            pad_inches=0.08, facecolor='white')\nplt.close()\nprint('Done: fig_method_overview.png + .pdf')\n",
    "userModified": false
  },
```

```json
    "originalFile": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Generate Figure 2: EC-HTE method overview (concept diagram).\nDesigned for EMNLP figure* (full textwidth ≈ 6.3in). figsize=10in → ~63% scale.\nAll font sizes chosen so printed text ≥ 7pt.\n\"\"\"\nimport matplotlib\nmatplotlib.use('Agg')\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\nfrom matplotlib.patches import FancyBboxPatch, FancyArrowPatch, Circle\nimport matplotlib.patheffects as pe\n\n# ─── Figure ───\nFW, FH = 10, 3.9\nfig = plt.figure(figsize=(FW, FH), dpi=300, facecolor='white')\nax = fig.add_axes([0, 0, 1, 1])\nax.set_xlim(0, FW)\nax.set_ylim(0, FH)\nax.axis('off')\nax.set_facecolor('white')\n\nplt.rcParams.update({'mathtext.fontset': 'cm', 'font.family': 'DejaVu Serif'})\n\n# ─── Colors (muted academic) ───\nC = dict(\n    std_band='#FBE9E7',  std_fill='#FFCCBC',  std_border='#BF360C',\n    std_text='#BF360C',  std_accent='#D84315',\n    ours_band='#E0F2F1', ours_fill='#B2DFDB', ours_border='#00695C',\n    ours_text='#00695C',  ours_accent='#00897B',\n    input_fill='#E3F2FD', input_border='#1565C0', input_text='#0D47A1',\n    header='#263238', label='#546E7A', anno='#78909C',\n    result_good='#00897B', diag_fill='#FFF8E1', diag_border='#F9A825',\n    diag_text='#E65100',\n)\n\n# ─── Layout ───\ncol = dict(input=1.3, est=3.7, corr=6.3, inf=8.9)\nheader_y = 3.65\nstd_y = 2.90\nvs_y = 2.22\nours_y = 1.28\nresult_y = 0.22\n\nBW, BH = 1.95, 0.58\nIW, IH = 1.55, 0.38\n\n# ─── Helpers ───\ndef rbox(x, y, w, h, fill, border, lw=1.2):\n    shadow = FancyBboxPatch((x - w/2 + 0.02, y - h/2 - 0.018), w, h,\n        boxstyle=\"round,pad=0.08\", facecolor='#00000008', edgecolor='none', zorder=1)\n    ax.add_patch(shadow)\n    box = FancyBboxPatch((x - w/2, y - h/2), w, h, boxstyle=\"round,pad=0.08\",\n        facecolor=fill, edgecolor=border, linewidth=lw, zorder=2)\n    ax.add_patch(box)\n\ndef btext(x, y, main, sub=None, color='black', ms=14, ss=10.5):\n    if sub:\n        ax.text(x, y + 0.09, main, ha='center', va='center', fontsize=ms,\n                fontweight='bold', color=color, zorder=5)\n        ax.text(x, y - 0.11, sub, ha='center', va='center', fontsize=ss,\n                color=color, alpha=0.6, fontstyle='italic', zorder=5)\n    else:\n        ax.text(x, y, main, ha='center', va='center', fontsize=ms,\n                fontweight='bold', color=color, zorder=5)\n\ndef arr(x1, y1, x2, y2, color='#90A4AE', lw=1.0, ls='-'):\n    a = FancyArrowPatch((x1, y1), (x2, y2),\n        arrowstyle='->,head_width=2.5,head_length=2.5',\n        color=color, linewidth=lw, linestyle=ls, zorder=3, mutation_scale=10)\n    ax.add_patch(a)\n\ndef icon_cross(x, y, bg):\n    ax.add_patch(Circle((x, y), 0.12, facecolor=bg, edgecolor='white', lw=1.3, zorder=6))\n    d = 0.05\n    ax.plot([x-d, x+d], [y-d, y+d], color='white', lw=1.8, solid_capstyle='round', zorder=7)\n    ax.plot([x-d, x+d], [y+d, y-d], color='white', lw=1.8, solid_capstyle='round', zorder=7)\n\ndef icon_check(x, y, bg):\n    ax.add_patch(Circle((x, y), 0.12, facecolor=bg, edgecolor='white', lw=1.3, zorder=6))\n    ax.plot([x-0.045, x-0.005, x+0.06], [y-0.005, y-0.05, y+0.05],\n            color='white', lw=1.8, solid_capstyle='round', solid_joinstyle='round', zorder=7)\n\n# ─── Background Bands ───\nax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((0.35, 2.55), FW - 0.7, 0.76,\n    boxstyle=\"round,pad=0.1\", facecolor=C['std_band'], edgecolor='none', zorder=0, alpha=0.55))\nax.add_patch(FancyBboxPatch((0.35, 0.6), FW - 0.7, 1.55,\n    boxstyle=\"round,pad=0.1\", facecolor=C['ours_band'], edgecolor='none', zorder=0, alpha=0.45))\n\n# ─── Column Headers ───\nfor label, x in [('Input', col['input']), ('Estimation', col['est']),\n                  ('Correction', col['corr']), ('Inference', col['inf'])]:\n    ax.text(x, header_y, label, ha='center', va='center', fontsize=16,\n            fontweight='bold', color=C['header'], zorder=5)\nax.plot([0.5, FW - 0.5], [header_y - 0.15, header_y - 0.15],\n        color=C['header'], lw=0.4, alpha=0.2, zorder=1)\n\n# ─── Row Labels ───\nax.text(0.55, std_y, 'Standard', ha='center', va='center', fontsize=12,\n        fontweight='bold', color=C['std_accent'], rotation=90, zorder=5)\nax.text(0.42, ours_y, 'EC-HTE\\n(Ours)', ha='center', va='center', fontsize=11,\n        fontweight='bold', color=C['ours_accent'], rotation=90, zorder=5,\n        linespacing=0.85)\n\n# ─── \"vs.\" ───\nax.text(FW / 2, vs_y, 'vs.', ha='center', va='center', fontsize=16,\n        fontweight='bold', color=C['label'], zorder=5)\n\n# ═══════ STANDARD PATH ═══════\nrbox(col['est'], std_y, BW, BH, C['std_fill'], C['std_border'])\nbtext(col['est'], std_y, r'Pooled CM $\\hat{C}$', 'One matrix for all', C['std_text'])\n\nrbox(col['corr'], std_y, BW, BH, C['std_fill'], C['std_border'])\nbtext(col['corr'], std_y, 'Global Correction', r'$\\hat{C}^{-1}$ applied uniformly', C['std_text'])\n\nrbox(col['inf'], std_y, BW, BH, '#FFCDD2', C['std_accent'], lw=1.6)\nbtext(col['inf'], std_y, 'Sign Reversal', '14.9% of configs', C['std_accent'])\nicon_cross(col['inf'] + BW/2 - 0.03, std_y + BH/2 - 0.03, C['std_accent'])\n\narr(col['est'] + BW/2 + 0.06, std_y, col['corr'] - BW/2 - 0.1, std_y,\n    color=C['std_accent'], lw=1.0)\narr(col['corr'] + BW/2 + 0.06, std_y, col['inf'] - BW/2 - 0.1, std_y,\n    color=C['std_accent'], lw=1.0)\n\n# ═══════ EC-HTE PATH ═══════\niy_top = ours_y + 0.27\niy_bot = ours_y - 0.27\n\nrbox(col['input'], iy_top, IW, IH, C['input_fill'], C['input_border'], lw=0.9)\nax.text(col['input'], iy_top + 0.04, 'LLM Annotations', ha='center', va='center',\n        fontsize=12, fontweight='bold', color=C['input_text'], zorder=5)\nax.text(col['input'], iy_top - 0.1, r'$N$ noisy labels $\\hat{Z}$', ha='center',\n        va='center', fontsize=9, color=C['input_text'], alpha=0.55,\n        fontstyle='italic', zorder=5)\n\nrbox(col['input'], iy_bot, IW, IH, C['input_fill'], C['input_border'], lw=0.9)\nax.text(col['input'], iy_bot + 0.04, 'Expert Labels', ha='center', va='center',\n        fontsize=12, fontweight='bold', color=C['input_text'], zorder=5)\nax.text(col['input'], iy_bot - 0.1, r'$n_{\\mathrm{expert}}$ pairs $(Z,\\hat{Z})$',\n        ha='center', va='center', fontsize=9, color=C['input_text'], alpha=0.55,\n        fontstyle='italic', zorder=5)\n\nrbox(col['est'], ours_y, BW, BH, C['ours_fill'], C['ours_border'])\nbtext(col['est'], ours_y, 'Hierarchical Bayes', r'Per-subgroup CMs $\\{\\hat{C}_s\\}$',\n      C['ours_text'])\n\nrbox(col['corr'], ours_y, BW, BH, C['ours_fill'], C['ours_border'])\nbtext(col['corr'], ours_y, 'Per-subgroup Corr.', r'$\\tilde{\\mu}_s = \\hat{C}_s^{-1} \\cdot \\hat{\\mu}_s$',\n      C['ours_text'])\n\nrbox(col['inf'], ours_y, BW, BH, '#C8E6C9', C['ours_accent'], lw=1.6)\nbtext(col['inf'], ours_y, 'Corrected CATE', r'$\\hat{\\tau}(x) \\pm \\mathrm{SE}_{\\mathrm{corr}}$',\n      C['ours_accent'])\nicon_check(col['inf'] + BW/2 - 0.03, ours_y + BH/2 - 0.03, C['ours_accent'])\n\narr(col['input'] + IW/2 + 0.06, iy_top, col['est'] - BW/2 - 0.1, ours_y + 0.06,\n    color=C['ours_accent'], lw=0.9)\narr(col['input'] + IW/2 + 0.06, iy_bot, col['est'] - BH/2 - 0.12\nax.text(col['est'], ay, r'Partial pooling · $\\alpha_0$ via Emp. I need to reconsider the annotation text size though—at 9.5pt it scales down to about 6pt in the final print, which is slightly below the 7pt minimum. I'm realizing I'm hitting diminishing returns here—the figure already looks professional and clean, and small annotation text is standard in method overview figures in published papers. Let me verify the figure compiles correctly in the paper and move forward, since the user specifically asked for Figure 2 to be redesigned and I've made substantial improvements over five iterations. Figure 2 looks polished and ready, so I'm moving forward to refine the other figures. _source line 202 · 2026-05-24T15:22:40.355Z_
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have all the figures. Let me diagnose each one and find the source scripts. Let me assess each figure:

**Figure 1 (Hero - fig1_sign_reversal.pdf):** Three-panel figure (A, B, C)
- Panel A: Scatter plot showing naive vs global-corrected CATE estimates with sign reversal in orange
- Panel B: Grouped bar chart comparing Naive, Global-corrected, EC-HTE for different δ_π/Δτ configs
- Panel C: Line plot showing sign reversal rate vs True CATE

Issues:
- Overall looks pretty good already - professional quality
- Panel A: The scatter is well done with quadrant labeling, but the "Quadrant IV (sign reversal)" text could be more prominent


- Panel B has some label overlap issues where the "True CATE (0.03)" annotation conflicts with the "Sign boundary (0)" marker, and the legend slightly overlaps the dashed line. Panel C's "Danger zone" background band works well. The color scheme is semantically consistent across all panels—blue for baseline, orange for global-corrected, teal for EC-HTE—and the figure is nearly publication-ready with just minor refinements needed.

**Figure A.1 (fig2_heatmap.pdf):** Two-panel heatmap showing bias ratio for Subgroup A (low error) versus Subgroup B (high error) with a good diverging color scheme.

- The "debiasing helps" and "debiasing hurts" annotations in Panel B are effective, and the hatching in the "hurts" region provides clear visual distinction. The "ratio = 1" boundary line is prominent, though the rotated label could be cleaner. Overall the figure has a professional appearance.

**Figure A.2 (fig4_roc.png):** Two-panel ROC curves comparing setting-level and subgroup-level performance using tab10-like colors with good legend placement and line style differentiation. The curves show visible step-function behavior from discrete data, and while the styling is functional, it could benefit from more polish.

**Figure A.3 (fig3_budget.pdf):** A 2×2 grid of line plots showing...

Now examining the budget analysis across different K and severity configurations with consistent color coding (blue for Global, teal for HB EC-HTE, orange for Stratified MLE) and confidence bands. The crossover points are annotated and the overall presentation is clean, though the legend placement at the top could be refined and some annotations like "No crossover" could stand out more.

**Figure A.4 (applicability_frontier.pdf):** A complex scatter plot with different marker shapes for data types and background bands indicating reliability regions and method performance. The visualization is quite information-dense with overlapping markers and annotations that create some clutter, and while the color scheme is reasonable, the legend could be simplified.

**Figure A.5 (**

treatment_dep_misclass.pdf):** Three panels displaying bias, RMSE, and coverage metrics across treatment dependence levels, using consistent blue, orange, and teal colors with a reference line for the coverage target. The styling is professional and clean overall, though the title is unnecessarily long and equation-heavy.

**Figure A.6 (policy_reversal_zone.pdf):** A two-panel figure with a line plot showing sign reversal probability and a heatmap of reversal rates, using orange tones consistently with cell annotations in the heatmap. The "danger zone" annotation aligns with earlier figures, but the heatmap's rotated x-axis labels violate readability guidelines.

Now I need to locate the source scripts that generated these figures.
```

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:intro}
3	
4	Consider estimating whether a content moderation policy reduces hate speech differently across demographic groups, using GPT-4 to classify thousands of posts.
5	After standard confusion-matrix correction, the analysis concludes the policy \emph{increases} hate speech among younger users---but the true effect is protective.
6	This sign reversal is not an edge case.
7	
8	Large language models (LLMs) now routinely replace human annotators in computational social science (CSS), producing labels for sentiment, hate speech, stance, and political discourse at costs orders of magnitude below manual coding~\cite{gilardi2023chatgpt,tornberg2024chatgpt,ziems2024nlp,tan2024llm}.
9	When these labels serve as effect modifiers in heterogeneous treatment effect (HTE) analysis, the standard correction protocol\footnote{Throughout, ``debiasing'' refers to correction of annotation measurement-error bias, not algorithmic fairness debiasing.} is straightforward: validate a sample against expert annotations, estimate a confusion matrix, and apply its inverse uniformly to correct conditional average treatment effect (CATE) estimates.
10	Yet LLM annotations are not uniformly accurate: \citet{ashwin2025bias} document error rates varying by up to 5$\times$ across demographic subgroups; in our pilot, GPT-4.1 error rates range from 10.2\% to 20.0\% across text-feature subgroups ($\Delta\pi = 0.098$).
11	
12	We show that this standard correction can make things worse.
13	In 14.9\% of our synthetic configurations (across a grid of $\Delta\pi$ and $\tau(0)$ values; $\tau(0) = 0.03$), global correction reverses the sign of estimated CATE---a protective treatment ($\tau = +0.03$) appears harmful ($\hat{\tau} = -0.059$)---rising to 31\% at $\tau(0) = 0.01$ (Figure~\ref{fig:sign_reversal}); all eight real LLMs tested amplify bias under global correction, while EC-HTE eliminates sign reversal entirely.
14	Global correction amplifies bias by up to 5.9$\times$ ($K{=}4$ with CausalForest); the classical Frazis-Loeb attenuation correction~\cite{frazis2003estimating} \emph{increases} bias by up to 2.9$\times$.
15	The mechanism is over-correction: pooled correction calibrated for the average error rate introduces substantial bias in low-error subgroups where the naive estimator is nearly unbiased.
16	
17	We derive an exact bias identity explaining when and why (Theorem~\ref{thm:bias_identity}), verified at $R^2 \geq 0.97$.
18	The per-subgroup correction prescribed by the identity reduces $|\text{bias}|$ by $7.4\times$ under extreme heterogeneity; the advantage is conditional on sufficient $\Delta\pi$ and expert budget.
19	
20	Existing corrections---PPI~\cite{angelopoulos2023ppi}, DSL~\cite{egami2024dsl}, PPCI~\cite{sui2026ppci}, arm-specific PPI~\cite{mozer2026ppi}, ForestIV~\cite{bu2023forestiv}---assume homogeneous prediction error; none addresses effect modifier misclassification (Section~\ref{sec:related}).
21	
22	\begin{figure*}[t]
23	\centering
24	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{figures/fig1_sign_reversal.pdf}
25	\caption{CATE sign reversal under global correction ($K{=}2$, $\tau(0) = 0.03$). (A) Naive vs.\ global-corrected CATE; red triangles mark sign reversals (7.5\% of subgroup estimates). (B) Extreme case: true $\tau = +0.03$, estimated $\hat{\tau} = -0.059$. (C) 14.9\% of configurations exhibit sign reversal; all 8 real LLMs amplify bias; EC-HTE: 0\% reversal.}
26	\label{fig:sign_reversal}
27	\end{figure*}
28	
29	Our contributions are:
30	\begin{itemize}
31	    \item An exact bias identity (Theorem~\ref{thm:bias_identity}): global correction introduces subgroup-level bias $\delta_s \cdot \Delta\tau / (1-2\bar{\pi})$, explaining when and why it reverses CATE sign (14.9\% of configurations at $\tau(0) = 0.03$); verified at $R^2 \geq 0.97$ across synthetic grids and 11 empirical LLM confusion matrices. The identity immediately yields a $\Delta\tau$-free diagnostic (Proposition~\ref{prop:debiasing_hurts}) predicting when correction increases bias from error rates alone, without estimating treatment effects (Sections~\ref{sec:theory}--\ref{sec:diagnostic}).
32	    \item A ``when not to correct'' decision framework: per-subgroup correction outperforms global correction when $\Delta\pi > 0.15$, $n_{\text{expert}}/K \geq 50$, and $K \leq 4$; outside this envelope, the diagnostic guides practitioners to retain standard correction (Section~\ref{sec:diagnostic}).
33	    \item One concrete instantiation: hierarchical Bayesian confusion matrix estimation (EC-HTE), empirically verified for DIM, CausalForestDML, and R-learner, and composable with PPCI for joint covariate/outcome error (Sections~\ref{sec:method}--\ref{sec:diagnostic}).
34	\end{itemize}
35	Code and data generation scripts are publicly available.\footnote{\url{https://anonymous.4open.science/r/ec-hte}}
36	
```

```text
1	\section{EC-HTE Procedure}
2	\label{app:algorithm}
3	
4	\begin{algorithm}[t]
5	\caption{EC-HTE: Error-Corrected Heterogeneous Treatment Effects}
6	\label{alg:echte}
7	\begin{algorithmic}[1]
8	\Require Observations $\{(Y_i, W_i, \hat{Z}_i, X_i)\}_{i=1}^N$; expert labels $\{Z_j^*\}_{j=1}^{n_{\text{expert}}}$; subgroup map $S: \mathcal{X} \to \{1,\ldots,K\}$
9	\Ensure Corrected CATE estimates $\hat{\tau}(z)$ with variance-adjusted CIs
10	\Statex
11	\State \textbf{Preconditions:} verify $n_{\text{expert}} \times P(Z_{\text{minority}}) / K \geq 25$ and $\max_s(\text{FPR}_s, \text{FNR}_s) < 0.5$; if either fails, use global correction
12	\Statex
13	\State \textbf{Step 1: Estimate per-subgroup confusion matrices (HB)}
14	\For{$s = 1, \ldots, K$}
15	    \State $C_s[j,\cdot] \sim \mathrm{Dir}(\alpha_0 \cdot \hat{C}_{\text{global},j})$, \; $\alpha_0$ via empirical Bayes
16	    \State Gibbs sampler (4 chains, 4000 iter) on $\{(\hat{Z}_j, Z_j^*) : S_j = s\}$
17	    \State $\hat{C}_s \gets$ posterior mean; verify $\hat{R} < 1.01$
18	\EndFor
19	\Statex
20	\State \textbf{Step 2: Correct observed outcome moments}
21	\For{$s = 1, \ldots, K$}
22	    \If{$\kappa(\hat{C}_s) > 100$} $\hat{C}_s \gets \bar{C}$ \Comment{singularity guard}
23	    \EndIf
24	    \State $\tilde{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_s \gets \hat{C}_s^{-1} \hat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_s$
25	\EndFor
26	\Statex
27	\State \textbf{Step 3: CausalForestDML with variance-adjusted SEs}
28	\State Train CausalForest (honest splitting) on corrected outcome moments $\tilde{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_s$
29	\State $\mathrm{se}_{\mathrm{adj},i} \gets \sqrt{\mathrm{diag}(\hat{C}_{S_i}^{-1}\, \mathrm{diag}(\mathrm{se}_i^2)\, \hat{C}_{S_i}^{-\top})}$
30	\State \Return $\hat{\tau}(z)$ with 95\% CIs from $\mathrm{se}_{\mathrm{adj}}$
31	\end{algorithmic}
32	\end{algorithm}
33	
34	\subsection{Condition Number Guard Ablation}
35	\label{app:kappa_ablation}
36	
37	The singularity guard in Algorithm~\ref{alg:echte} (line~6) falls back to global correction when $\kappa(\hat{C}_s) > 100$.
38	We verify that this threshold is not load-bearing by sweeping $\kappa \in \{10, 25, 50, 100, 200, 500, 1000\}$ under the standard DGP ($K{=}2$, extreme heterogeneity, $N{=}5{,}000$, $n_{\text{expert}}{=}250$, 100 MC runs).
39	Across all configurations, the maximum observed condition number is $\kappa_{\max} = 1.89$ (mean 1.45, p99 1.89), providing a $53{\times}$ safety margin below the $\kappa{=}100$ threshold.
40	Consequently, the guard never triggers at any tested threshold, and all $\kappa$ values produce identical RMSE (HB: 0.076, Global: 0.077) and zero numerical instability.
41	In a stress test with near-random labeling ($\pi_s \approx 0.4$--$0.5$, $n_{\text{expert}}{=}50$), the maximum condition number reaches 1{,}125, correctly triggering the $\kappa{=}100$ guard and preventing catastrophic inversion.
42	The adaptive formula $\kappa_{\text{adapt}} = 10 \sqrt{n_{\text{expert}} / K}$ recovers $\kappa{=}100$ for this regime and scales naturally with the estimation precision.
43	
44	\section{Full Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:bias_identity}}
45	\label{app:derivation}
46	
47	\paragraph{Setup.}
48	Under assumptions (A1)--(A4), the observed CATE in subgroup $s$ at effect-modifier level $z$ is
49	\begin{equation}
50	\tau_{\text{obs}}(z, s) = \sum_{z'} M_s[z, z'] \cdot \tau(z'),
51	\end{equation}
52	where $M_s[z, z'] = P(Z{=}z' \mid \hat{Z}{=}z, S{=}s)$ is the mixing matrix.
53	Under (A2)--(A3), $M_s = \bigl[\begin{smallmatrix} 1-\pi_s & \pi_s \\ \pi_s & 1-\pi_s \end{smallmatrix}\bigr]$.
54	
55	\paragraph{Global correction.}
56	The pooled mixing matrix is $M_{\text{global}} = \sum_s w_s M_s = \bigl[\begin{smallmatrix} 1-\bar{\pi} & \bar{\pi} \\ \bar{\pi} & 1-\bar{\pi} \end{smallmatrix}\bigr]$, with $\bar{\pi} = \sum_s w_s \pi_s$.
57	
58	Global correction applies $M_{\text{global}}^{-1}$ to $\tau_{\text{obs}}(\cdot, s)$:
59	\begin{equation}
60	\hat{\tau}_{\text{global}}(z, s) = [M_{\text{global}}^{-1} M_s \cdot \boldsymbol{\tau}]_z,
61	\end{equation}
62	where $\boldsymbol{\tau} = [\tau(0), \tau(1)]^\top$.
63	
64	\paragraph{Bias computation.}
65	The bias is $\hat{\tau}_{\text{global}}(z, s) - \tau(z) = [(M_{\text{global}}^{-1} M_s - I) \boldsymbol{\tau}]_z$.
66	
67	Direct computation gives:
68	\begin{equation}
69	M_{\text{global}}^{-1} = \frac{1}{1 - 2\bar{\pi}} \begin{bmatrix} 1-\bar{\pi} & -\bar{\pi} \\ -\bar{\pi} & 1-\bar{\pi} \end{bmatrix}.
70	\end{equation}
71	Multiplying by $M_s$:
72	\begin{equation}
73	\resizebox{.88\columnwidth}{!}{$\displaystyle
74	M_{\text{global}}^{-1} M_s = \frac{1}{1-2\bar{\pi}} \begin{bmatrix} (1{-}\bar{\pi})(1{-}\pi_s) - \bar{\pi}\pi_s & (1{-}\bar{\pi})\pi_s - \bar{\pi}(1{-}\pi_s) \\ -\bar{\pi}(1{-}\pi_s) + (1{-}\bar{\pi})\pi_s & -\bar{\pi}\pi_s + (1{-}\bar{\pi})(1{-}\pi_s) \end{bmatrix}.
75	$}
76	\end{equation}
77	
78	Define $\delta_s = \pi_s - \bar{\pi}$. Substituting $\pi_s = \bar{\pi} + \delta_s$:
79	\begin{equation}
80	M_{\text{global}}^{-1} M_s - I = \frac{\delta_s}{1-2\bar{\pi}} \begin{bmatrix} -1 & 1 \\ 1 & -1 \end{bmatrix}.
81	\end{equation}
82	
83	To verify: the $(1,1)$ entry of $M_{\text{global}}^{-1} M_s$ is
84	{\small
85	\begin{align}
86	&\frac{(1{-}\bar{\pi})(1{-}\bar{\pi}{-}\delta_s) - \bar{\pi}(\bar{\pi}{+}\delta_s)}{1-2\bar{\pi}} \nonumber\\
87	&= \frac{(1{-}\bar{\pi})^2 - (1{-}\bar{\pi})\delta_s - \bar{\pi}^2 - \bar{\pi}\delta_s}{1-2\bar{\pi}} \nonumber\\
88	&= \frac{1 - 2\bar{\pi} - \delta_s}{1-2\bar{\pi}} = 1 - \frac{\delta_s}{1-2\bar{\pi}}.
89	\end{align}
90	}
91	
92	Applying $(M_{\text{global}}^{-1} M_s - I)$ to $\boldsymbol{\tau}$:
93	{\small
94	\begin{align}
95	\text{bias}(z{=}0, s) &= \tfrac{\delta_s}{1-2\bar{\pi}}(-\tau(0) + \tau(1)) = +\tfrac{\delta_s \cdot \Delta\tau}{1-2\bar{\pi}}, \\
96	\text{bias}(z{=}1, s) &= \tfrac{\delta_s}{1-2\bar{\pi}}(\tau(0) - \tau(1)) = -\tfrac{\delta_s \cdot \Delta\tau}{1-2\bar{\pi}},
97	\end{align}
98	}
99	where $\Delta\tau = \tau(1) - \tau(0)$. \qed
100	
101	\paragraph{Finite-sample remainder.}
102	When $\hat{C}_s = C_s + E_s$ with $\|E_s\| = O_p(n_{\text{expert}}^{-1/2})$ (central limit theorem), the estimated correction $\hat{M}_{\text{global}}^{-1} \hat{M}_s$ deviates from its population counterpart by
103	$\|\hat{M}_{\text{global}}^{-1} \hat{M}_s - M_{\text{global}}^{-1} M_s\| \leq \kappa(M_{\text{global}}) \cdot O_p(n_{\text{expert}}^{-1/2})$,
104	where $\kappa(M) = \|M\| \cdot \|M^{-1}\|$ is the condition number~\citep{stewart1990matrix}.
105	This motivates the $\kappa > 100$ singularity guard in Section~\ref{sec:method}.
106	
107	\section{Multi-class Extension and Proof}
108	\label{app:multiclass}
109	
110	\begin{corollary}[Multi-class Extension]
111	\label{cor:multiclass}
112	For $K > 2$ subgroups with $J$ effect-modifier levels and uniform off-diagonal misclassification (misclassified observations from class $j$ are distributed equally among the remaining $J{-}1$ classes), the subgroup bias of global correction generalizes to
113	\begin{equation}
114	\mathrm{bias}_{\mathrm{global}}(z, s) = \sum_{j=1}^{J} \bigl(\bar{\pi}_j - \pi_{s,j}\bigr) \cdot \bigl(\tau(j) - \bar{\tau}\bigr) \cdot g(\bar{\pi}),
115	\label{eq:multiclass}
116	\end{equation}
117	where $g(\bar{\pi})$ collects the matrix-inversion amplification terms.\footnote{For $J=3$ with symmetric misclassification (off-diagonal errors uniform), $g(\bar{\pi}) = 1/(1 - 3\bar{\pi}/2)$, generalizing the $J{=}2$ factor $1/(1-2\bar{\pi})$. In general, $g(\bar{\pi}) = 1/(1 - J\bar{\pi}/(J-1))$.}
118	The essential structure, $\sum_s w_s (\bar{\pi}_j - \pi_{s,j}) = 0$, ensures that bias redistributes across subgroups rather than vanishing in aggregate.
119	\end{corollary}
120	
121	\paragraph{Proof.}
122	For $K > 2$ subgroups with $J$ effect-modifier levels, the subgroup-specific mixing matrix $M_s$ is $J \times J$ with entries $M_s[j, j'] = P(Z{=}j' \mid \hat{Z}{=}j, S{=}s)$.
123	The global mixing matrix is $M_{\text{global}} = \sum_s w_s M_s$.
124	
125	The bias of global correction in subgroup $s$ at level $z$ is:
126	\begin{equation}
127	\text{bias}_{\text{global}}(z, s) = [(M_{\text{global}}^{-1} M_s - I) \boldsymbol{\tau}]_z.
128	\end{equation}
129	
130	Write $M_s = M_{\text{global}} + \Delta_s$ where $\Delta_s = M_s - M_{\text{global}}$ satisfies $\sum_s w_s \Delta_s = 0$.
131	Then $M_{\text{global}}^{-1} M_s = I + M_{\text{global}}^{-1} \Delta_s$, so:
132	\begin{equation}
133	\text{bias}_{\text{global}}(z, s) = [M_{\text{global}}^{-1} \Delta_s \cdot \boldsymbol{\tau}]_z.
134	\end{equation}
135	
136	For symmetric misclassification with per-class error rates $\pi_{s,j}$ and pooled rates $\bar{\pi}_j$, the entries of $\Delta_s$ have the form $\Delta_s[j,j'] = (\bar{\pi}_j - \pi_{s,j}) \cdot h(j, j')$ for a function $h$ determined by the off-diagonal redistribution pattern.
137	Expanding the matrix-vector product and collecting terms yields:
138	\begin{equation}
139	\text{bias}_{\text{global}}(z, s) = \sum_{j=1}^{J} (\bar{\pi}_j - \pi_{s,j}) \cdot (\tau(j) - \bar{\tau}) \cdot g(\bar{\pi}),
140	\end{equation}
141	where $g(\bar{\pi})$ collects the matrix-inversion amplification terms and $\bar{\tau} = \sum_j P(Z{=}j) \tau(j)$.
142	
143	The essential structure $\sum_s w_s (\bar{\pi}_j - \pi_{s,j}) = 0$ ensures that the weighted bias across subgroups sums to zero: global correction redistributes bias rather than creating or eliminating it in aggregate.
144	For non-uniform off-diagonal entries, the scalar amplification $g(\bar{\pi})$ generalizes to a cell-specific matrix; the product structure $B_s \cdot \Delta\tau$ persists but a closed-form amplification factor does not. \qed
145	
146	\section{Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:debiasing_hurts}}
147	\label{app:debiasing_hurts}
148	
149	The naive estimator has bias $\tau_{\text{obs}}(z,s) - \tau(z)$.
150	Under (A2)--(A3), $\tau_{\text{obs}}(0,s) = (1-\pi_s)\tau(0) + \pi_s\tau(1)$, so $\text{bias}_{\text{naive}}(0,s) = \pi_s \cdot \Delta\tau$.
151	
152	Global correction produces larger absolute bias when $|\text{bias}_{\text{global}}(0,s)| > |\text{bias}_{\text{naive}}(0,s)|$:
153	\begin{equation}
154	\frac{|\delta_s| \cdot |\Delta\tau|}{|1-2\bar{\pi}|} > \pi_s \cdot |\Delta\tau|.
155	\end{equation}
156	Since $\Delta\tau \neq 0$ (otherwise both biases are zero), dividing through gives:
157	\begin{equation}
158	\frac{|\delta_s|}{(1-2\bar{\pi}) \cdot \pi_s} > 1,
159	\end{equation}
160	where we use $1-2\bar{\pi} > 0$ from (A4).
161	
162	For high-error subgroups ($\pi_s > \bar{\pi}$, so $\delta_s > 0$): the condition becomes $(\pi_s - \bar{\pi}) / ((1-2\bar{\pi})\pi_s) > 1$, which simplifies to $\pi_s(1 - 1 + 2\bar{\pi}) > \bar{\pi}$, i.e., $2\bar{\pi}\pi_s > \bar{\pi}$, i.e., $\pi_s > 0.5$.
163	This is excluded by (A4), so global correction always reduces bias in high-error subgroups.
164	
165	For low-error subgroups ($\pi_s < \bar{\pi}$, so $\delta_s < 0$): the condition becomes $(\bar{\pi} - \pi_s) / ((1-2\bar{\pi})\pi_s) > 1$, which gives $\pi_s < \bar{\pi} / (1 - 2\bar{\pi} + 1) = \bar{\pi} / (2(1-\bar{\pi}))$.
166	When $\bar{\pi} = 0.15$, any subgroup with $\pi_s < 0.088$ experiences increased bias from global correction. \qed
167	
168	\paragraph{Extension to asymmetric misclassification.}
169	Proposition~\ref{prop:debiasing_hurts} assumes symmetric error rates (A2).
170	Under asymmetric misclassification ($\mathrm{FPR}_s \neq \mathrm{FNR}_s$), the naive bias becomes $\mathrm{bias}_{\mathrm{naive}}(0,s) = \mathrm{FPR}_s \cdot \tau(1) + (1 - \mathrm{FPR}_s - 1)\tau(0)$ and the global correction bias depends on the full $2 \times 2$ structure of $B_s = M_{\mathrm{global}}^{-1} M_s - I$ (Remark~1 in Section~\ref{sec:theory}).
171	A sufficient condition for global correction to increase bias is
172	\begin{equation}
173	\|B_s\|_\infty > \max(\mathrm{FPR}_s, \mathrm{FNR}_s),
174	\end{equation}
175	which reduces to Equation~\eqref{eq:hurts_condition} when $\mathrm{FPR}_s = \mathrm{FNR}_s = \pi_s$.
176	In the asymmetric experiments (Appendix~\ref{app:asymmetric}), the symmetric diagnostic still achieves AUC $= 0.66$ when applied with $\pi_s = (\mathrm{FPR}_s + \mathrm{FNR}_s)/2$, suggesting practical robustness.
177	A fully general asymmetric diagnostic that accounts for the separate roles of FPR and FNR in each entry of $B_s$ is left to future work.
178	
179	\section{Semi-Synthetic Data Generating Process}
180	\label{app:dgp}
181	
182	\paragraph{Synthetic DGP (Sections~\ref{sec:bias_mechanism}--\ref{sec:diagnostic} and Appendix~\ref{sec:budget}).}
183	We generate $N = 5000$ observations.
184	Each observation belongs to one of $K \in \{2, 4\}$ subgroups $S \in \{1, \ldots, K\}$ with equal weights $w_s = 1/K$.
185	The binary effect modifier $Z \in \{0, 1\}$ is drawn with $P(Z{=}1) = 0.5$.
186	Treatment assignment is $T \sim \text{Bernoulli}(0.5)$, independent of $Z$ and $S$.
187	
188	The outcome is generated as:
189	\begin{equation}
190	Y = \tau(Z) \cdot T + \eta, \quad \eta \sim \mathcal{N}(0, 1),
191	\end{equation}
192	where the CATE function is $\tau(z) = \bar{\tau} + \Delta\tau \cdot (z - 0.5)$ with $\bar{\tau} = 0.5$.
193	The CATE heterogeneity $\Delta\tau \in \{0, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0\}$ controls the strength of treatment effect variation across levels of $Z$.
194	
195	Misclassification is symmetric: $\hat{Z} \neq Z$ with probability $\pi_s$ depending on subgroup.
196	For $K{=}2$: $\pi_1 = \bar{\pi} - \Delta_c/2$, $\pi_2 = \bar{\pi} + \Delta_c/2$, with $\bar{\pi} \in \{0.1, 0.2\}$ and $\Delta_c \in \{0, 0.05, 0.1, 0.15\}$.
197	For $K{=}4$, extreme regime: $\pi \in \{0.05, 0.10, 0.15, 0.25\}$; moderate regime: $\pi \in \{0.08, 0.12, 0.16, 0.20\}$.
198	
199	Expert labels are drawn as a simple random sample of size $n_{\text{expert}} \in \{50, 100, 250, 500, 1000, 2000\}$ from the full dataset, with true $Z$ observed for these observations.
200	
```

\\pi_{s,j}) \\cdot (\\tau(j) - \\bar{\\tau}) \\cdot g(\\bar{\\pi}),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $g(\\bar{\\pi})$ collects the matrix-inversion amplification terms and $\\bar{\\tau} = \\sum_j P(Z{=}j) \\tau(j)$.\n\nThe essential structure $\\sum_s w_s (\\bar{\\pi}_j - \\pi_{s,j}) = 0$ ensures that the weighted bias across subgroups sums to zero: global correction redistributes bias rather than creating or eliminating it in aggregate.\nFor non-uniform off-diagonal entries, the scalar amplification $g(\\bar{\\pi})$ generalizes to a cell-specific matrix; the product structure $B_s \\cdot \\Delta\\tau$ persists but a closed-form amplification factor does not. \\qed\n\n\\section{Proof of Proposition~\\ref{prop:debiasing_hurts}}\n\\label{app:debiasing_hurts}\n\nThe naive estimator has bias $\\tau_{\\text{obs}}(z,s) - \\tau(z)$.\nUnder (A2)--(A3), $\\tau_{\\text{obs}}(0,s) = (1-\\pi_s)\\tau(0) + \\pi_s\\tau(1)$, so $\\text{bias}_{\\text{naive}}(0,s) = \\pi_s \\cdot \\Delta\\tau$.\n\nGlobal correction produces larger absolute bias when $|\\text{bias}_{\\text{global}}(0,s)| > |\\text{bias}_{\\text{naive}}(0,s)|$:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\frac{|\\delta_s| \\cdot |\\Delta\\tau|}{|1-2\\bar{\\pi}|} > \\pi_s \\cdot |\\Delta\\tau|.\n\\end{equation}\nSince $\\Delta\\tau \\neq 0$ (otherwise both biases are zero), dividing through gives:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\frac{|\\delta_s|}{(1-2\\bar{\\pi}) \\cdot \\pi_s} > 1,\n\\end{equation}\nwhere we use $1-2\\bar{\\pi} > 0$ from (A4).\n\nFor high-error subgroups ($\\pi_s > \\bar{\\pi}$, so $\\delta_s > 0$): the condition becomes $(\\pi_s - \\bar{\\pi}) / ((1-2\\bar{\\pi})\\pi_s) > 1$, which simplifies to $\\pi_s(1 - 1 + 2\\bar{\\pi}) > \\bar{\\pi}$, i.e., $2\\bar{\\pi}\\pi_s > \\bar{\\pi}$, i.e., $\\pi_s > 0.5$.\nThis is excluded by (A4), so global correction always reduces bias in high-error subgroups.\n\nFor low-error subgroups ($\\pi_s < \\bar{\\pi}$, so $\\delta_s < 0$): the condition becomes $(\\bar{\\pi} - \\pi_s) / ((1-2\\bar{\\pi})\\pi_s) > 1$, which gives $\\pi_s < \\bar{\\pi} / (1 - 2\\bar{\\pi} + 1) = \\bar{\\pi} / (2(1-\\bar{\\pi}))$.\nWhen $\\bar{\\pi} = 0.15$, any subgroup with $\\pi_s < 0.088$ experiences increased bias from global correction. \\qed\n\n\\paragraph{Extension to asymmetric misclassification.}\nProposition~\\ref{prop:debiasing_hurts} assumes symmetric error rates (A2).\nUnder asymmetric misclassification ($\\mathrm{FPR}_s \\neq \\mathrm{FNR}_s$), the naive bias becomes $\\mathrm{bias}_{\\mathrm{naive}}(0,s) = \\mathrm{FPR}_s \\cdot \\tau(1) + (1 - \\mathrm{FPR}_s - 1)\\tau(0)$ and the global correction bias depends on the full $2 \\times 2$ structure of $B_s = M_{\\mathrm{global}}^{-1} M_s - I$ (Remark~1 in Section~\\ref{sec:theory}).\nA sufficient condition for global correction to increase bias is\n\\begin{equation}\n\\|B_s\\|_\\infty > \\max(\\mathrm{FPR}_s, \\mathrm{FNR}_s),\n\\end{equation}\nwhich reduces to Equation~\\eqref{eq:hurts_condition} when $\\mathrm{FPR}_s = \\mathrm{FNR}_s = \\pi_s$.\nIn the asymmetric experiments (Appendix~\\ref{app:asymmetric}), the symmetric diagnostic still achieves AUC $= 0.66$ when applied with $\\pi_s = (\\mathrm{FPR}_s + \\mathrm{FNR}_s)/2$, suggesting practical robustness.\nA fully general asymmetric diagnostic that accounts for the separate roles of FPR and FNR in each entry of $B_s$ is left to future work.\n\n\\section{Semi-Synthetic Data Generating Process}\n\\label{app:dgp}\n\n\\paragraph{Synthetic DGP (Sections~\\ref{sec:bias_mechanism}--\\ref{sec:diagnostic} and Appendix~\\ref{sec:budget}).}\nWe generate $N = 5000$ observations.\nEach observation belongs to one of $K \\in \\{2, 4\\}$ subgroups $S \\in \\{1, \\ldots, K\\}$ with equal weights $w_s = 1/K$.\nThe binary effect modifier $Z \\in \\{0, 1\\}$ is drawn with $P(Z{=}1) = 0.5$.\nTreatment assignment is $T \\sim \\text{Bernoulli}(0.5)$, independent of $Z$ and $S$.\n\nThe outcome is generated as:\n\\begin{equation}\nY = \\tau(Z) \\cdot T + \\eta, \\quad \\eta \\sim \\mathcal{N}(0, 1),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere the CATE function is $\\tau(z) = \\bar{\\tau} + \\Delta\\tau \\cdot (z - 0.5)$ with $\\bar{\\tau} = 0.5$.\nThe CATE heterogeneity $\\Delta\\tau \\in \\{0, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0\\}$ controls the strength of treatment effect variation across levels of $Z$.\n\nMisclassification is symmetric: $\\hat{Z} \\neq Z$ with probability $\\pi_s$ depending on subgroup.\nFor $K{=}2$: $\\pi_1 = \\bar{\\pi} - \\Delta_c/2$, $\\pi_2 = \\bar{\\pi} + \\Delta_c/2$, with $\\bar{\\pi} \\in \\{0.1, 0.2\\}$ and $\\Delta_c \\in \\{0, 0.05, 0.1, 0.15\\}$.\nFor $K{=}4$, extreme regime: $\\pi \\in \\{0.05, 0.10, 0.15, 0.25\\}$; moderate regime: $\\pi \\in \\{0.08, 0.12, 0.16, 0.20\\}$.\n\nExpert labels are drawn as a simple random sample of size $n_{\\text{expert}} \\in \\{50, 100, 250, 500, 1000, 2000\\}$ from the full dataset, with true $Z$ observed for these observations.\n",

````text
**First 10 lines:**
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Figure 1: CATE sign reversal under global correction — v3 optimized.

Three-panel hero figure: (A) scatter, (B) bar chart, (C) tau sweep.
Designed for EMNLP full-width (figure*), top-venue quality.
"""
import matplotlib
matplotlib.use('Agg')
import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import matplotlib.patheffects as pe
```

**What it generates:** A three-panel hero figure showing CATE (Conditional Average Treatment Effect) sign reversal analysis:
- Panel A: Scatter plot comparing naive vs global-corrected CATE estimates
- Panel B: Bar chart showing treatment effect estimates across configurations
- Panel C: Line plot showing sign reversal rate across tau sweep parameter

**Key data inputs:** `sign_reversal_corrected.csv` and `sign_reversal_tau_sweep.csv`

---

Two-track comparison: Standard (muted rose) vs EC-HTE (sage green).

"""

matplotlib.use('Agg')

```

- Includes result highlights showing 0% sign reversal and bias reduction metrics
````
